## COM337 — We asked 500 Housekeepers_Spanish
_(Second row titled COM337, created 2026-10-20.)_
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Iteration · **Format:** AI + Existing Footage · **Angle:** Housekeepers · **Persona:** Housekeepers · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** No Offer · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-20 · **Learnings:** —

Same script as COM331 but in Spanish

---

### Hook 1

Les hicimos una pregunta muy simple a 500 mujeres que trabajan en limpieza:

¿Qué buscan al comprar zapatos?

### Hook 2

Si te dedicas a la limpieza, deja de aceptar consejos sobre calzado de personas que pasan su jornada laboral sentadas.

Les preguntamos a 300 personas que trabajan en limpieza qué tipo de calzado prefieren.

### Hook 3

Si trabajás en limpieza, los zapatos equivocados pueden hacer que un turno de 8 horas se sienta como uno de 16.

Entrevistamos a 400 personas que trabajan en limpieza sobre el calzado que usan para trabajar.

### Body

Las mismas respuestas se repetían una y otra vez.

Comodidad, soporte, y zapatos que pudieran usar durante todo el turno.

Porque limpiar una habitación es una cosa.

Pero limpiar habitaciones, subir escaleras y recorrer pasillo tras pasillo durante ocho horas es otra muy distinta.

Una trabajadora nos contó que da más de 20.000 pasos por día.

Otra dijo que pasa entre ocho y diez horas de pie.

Y aproximadamente una de cada tres personas de este grupo declaró tener fascitis plantar.

Así que eso de que “son cómodos a la primera puesta” no alcanza.

La verdadera pregunta es: ¿cómo se sienten tus pies cuando todavía te quedan tres horas en tu turno de trabajo?

Primero, Skechers. Alrededor de 100 dólares.

Una marca conocida. Muchos modelos. Y una opción fácil para empezar cuando buscas calzado cómodo para trabajar.

Pero el punto débil que describen algunas personas que trabajan en limpieza aparece después.

Una trabajadora dijo que los suyos se sentían bien al principio. Después de un par de meses, tenía dolor en los talones y los pies, incluso fuera del trabajo.

Otra persona del sector dijo que sus Skechers se desgastaron rápido.

Necesitamos que esa comodidad sea duradera.

Después, Hoka. Alrededor de 175 dólares.

Su suela gruesa los convierte en una opción interesante cuando se trata de dolor de pies.

Pero más amortiguación no garantiza que sea el calzado adecuado…

Una trabajadora de nuestra encuesta describió sus Hoka como pesados. Y en una conversación sobre limpieza en Reddit, otra dijo que los suyos le dejaban los pies doloridos después de cinco horas.

Esa suela voluminosa puede sentirse como una nube al principio, pero algunas personas la sienten menos estable al pisar. Y cuando pasás el día yendo de una habitación a otra y subiendo escaleras, un ajuste firme y un buen agarre importan.

Después está New Balance. Alrededor de 160 dólares.

Otra marca que aparece en las conversaciones y que a muchas personas les gusta.

Pero algunos trabajadores todavía necesitan agregarles algo para que el calzado se sienta bien.

Plantillas adicionales. Más amortiguación en el talón. Otro par de plantillas de reemplazo cuando se desgastan.

Hasta nos han dicho que reemplazan las plantillas de sus New Balance de trabajo dos veces al año. Otro gasto recurrente que se suma al precio de los zapatos.

Entonces, ¿qué funciona realmente?

Después de revisar cientos de reseñas en Reddit y hablar con personas que trabajan en limpieza y pasan horas y horas de pie, la misma respuesta aparecía una y otra vez.

ComfortWear.

No es el calzado más elegante ni el más caro, pero sí el que seguía dando batalla después de la quinta hora de trabajo.

Una puntera amplia les da espacio a los dedos en lugar de apretarlos, especialmente cuando sientes los pies hinchados después de un día largo.

La parte superior elastizada se adapta al pie para ofrecer un ajuste más flexible.

Además, el soporte de arco incorporado y la amortiguación en el talón combinan soporte y suavidad bajo tus pies.

La mayoría de los zapatos populares fueron diseñados para correr, hacer ejercicio o usarlos de manera casual.

ComfortWear está diseñado para mujeres trabajadoras que tienen que pasar horas de pie.

Porque cuando llegan a casa, el día no siempre termina. Hay que preparar la cena. Ayudar a los chicos con la tarea. Hacer los mandados que todavía quedan pendientes.

Ahora, ComfortWear tiene un 40% de descuento, con envío gratis y 30 días para probarlos. Tiempo más que suficiente para ver cómo se sienten en tu rutina diaria.

Tu necesidad de comodidad no termina cuando termina tu turno.

Tocá abajo para encontrar tu talle y probar ComfortWear.

## COM338 — Husband tired of nurse wife complaining about foot pain drama
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Net New · **Format:** AI · **Angle:** Nurse · **Persona:** Nurse · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** No Offer · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-16 · **Learnings:** —

<b>“WHATEVER’S LEFT” — ComfortWear Nurse Drama</b>

<i>\[Hospital corridor. Late evening. No music. Sarah falls and another nurse catches her right on time </i>

<b>COLLEAGUE:</b>

Sarah? Sarah, are you ok??

<b>SARAH:</b>

I’m fine, just help me get up, I’ve got 4 patients waiting in ICU

<b>COLLEAGUE:</b>

No, you need to be seen by a doctor

<b>SARAH:</b>

No, no, i’m ok; they need me in twelve. It’s just my feet, i’ll be fine.

<i>\[She holds the counter while trying to get up. She’s wearing really beat up Hoka shoes\]</i>

<b>SARAH — VOICEOVER:</b>

I could tell when a patient was hurting before they said a word.

With myself?

I kept saying the same thing.

Just get through the shift.

<i>\[Cut to her parked car. She sits motionless, keys in her hand. Her phone lights up: “Still up for dinner? ❤️”\]</i>

---

<i>\[Home. Her husband, Mark, is dressed to go out. Sarah enters in scrubs and drops her bag.\]</i>

<b>MARK:</b>

Hey. Reservation’s at eight.

<b>SARAH:</b>

Can we just order something?

<i>\[His smile fades.\]</i>

<b>MARK:</b>

Again?

<b>SARAH:</b>

Please. My feet are killing me.

<i>\[She lowers herself onto the couch, carefully removing one shoe.\]</i>

<b>MARK:</b>

Dinner tonight. The market last weekend. That walk you wanted to take—

<b>SARAH:</b>

You think I don’t want to go?

<b>MARK:</b>

I don’t know anymore. We don’t do anything.

<b>SARAH:</b>

I’ve been on my feet twelve hours for 4 days in a row, Mark. I barely sat down to eat.

<b>MARK:</b>

I know.

<b>SARAH:</b>

Then why are you making me feel guilty?

<i>\[Silence. He sits opposite her. His voice softens.\]</i>

<b>MARK:</b>

I miss you.

And I hate watching you come home like this. We haven’t done anything in years. I’m depressed and unhappy.

<i>\[She looks down at her Hoka shoes, completely worn out.\]</i>

<b>SARAH:</b>

I don’t know what to do, my feet are always in pain. I’m struggling; I feel like quitting, but we need the money. Lizzie won’t be able to go to her favorite school if I quit

<b>MARK:</b>

When was the last time you had your feet looked at?

<b>SARAH:</b>

This is just what every nurse goes through. Everyone in my station complains about their feet.

<b>MARK:</b>

You’re holding onto the wall to get to our bathroom. Are they also doing that?

<i>\[She looks away.\]</i>

<b>SARAH:</b>

No.<br /><br /><b>MARK:</b><br /><br />Let me book you with the Podiatry Clinic in Charleston. I heard they’ve got a great team.<br /><br /><b>SARAH:<br /><br /></b>Sure, thanks <br /><br />\[Looks exhausted, acts as if it won’t make a difference\]<br /><br />\[Mark puts the divorce papers behind his back\]

---

<i>\[Podiatrist’s office. After an examination, Sarah wearing her nurse scrubs sits beside her Hoka work shoes.\]</i>

<b>PODIATRIST:</b>

So what’s going on, Sarah? Tell me what happens toward the end of a shift. Where are you feeling this pain?<br /><br />\[Podiatrist holding sarah’s feet, feeling each part of it, shoes are on the floor\]

<b>SARAH:</b>

This heel area hurts a lot. Feels like it’s ripped at times. My toe also swells up a lot. I start looking for somewhere to lean by hour 8. By hour 12, I’m barely standing.

Then I get home and the only thing I want to do is sit on the couch.

<b>PODIATRIST:</b>

And how long have you been working around that?

<b>SARAH:<br /><br /></b>5 years.

My husband stopped asking me to do things. It’s taking a big toll on our marriage.<br /><br />I’m getting anxious just thinking about work.

<i>\[A beat.\]</i>

<b>PODIATRIST:</b>

I’m glad you came in. We need to address the pain, and part of that conversation is what you’re wearing all day. I see you’ve got the Hokas on.

<b>SARAH:</b>

I bought these because they felt comfortable at the store. It’s only been 2 months since I‘ve had these, but they haven’t made any changes to my pain. <br /><br />I wore Skechers and Crocs before this. They felt even worse.

<b>PODIATRIST:</b>

I can see the problem already.<br /><br />Hokas are cushiony, but the cushion is doing you a disservice. You see, too much cushioning forces your feet to change the way they walk. It’s called overpronation. <br /><br />Now, your feet are already in a bad position. And on top of that, your toes are rubbing against the narrow toe-box that these shoes have. <br /><br />You can see how these shoes are triangle-shaped. 

Now, let’s look at your feet, not triangle-shaped. Much wider.

What do they feel like halfway through your shift?

<b>SARAH:</b>

Like I’m counting the minutes until I can take them off.

<b>PODIATRIST:</b>

That’s the part we need to pay attention to.

For long shifts, I look at room for the toes, support under the arch, cushioning, weight, and how securely the shoe fits.

<b>SARAH:</b>

So what do I do? I’ve tried the shoe all the nurses are talking about. Hokas, Skechers, Crocs, OnClouds. They still don’t help. These cost me a fortune, and we’re already behind on rent.

<b>PODIATRIST:<br /><br /></b>\[Podiatrist brings ComfortWear to her from the back area of the office\]

This is the ComfortWear Shoe. Our clinic received a batch of these a few months ago. <br /><br />They were designed by a team of podiatrists and retired nurses. The owner’s an old friend, so he’s been sending me a few boxes every month. <br /><br />All the nurses I have seen lately have been bringing these back home. No complaints so far.<br /><br />Let’s look at the fit and features.

Look at the front.

<b>SARAH:</b>

It's wide and looks stretchy.

<b>PODIATRIST:</b>

These give your toes room without pressing against them.

<b>SARAH:</b>

That’s where mine start feeling tight.

<b>PODIATRIST:</b>

Underneath, there’s a memory foam insole with arch support and cushioning for those long stretches of standing and walking.<br /><br />They’re also removable in case you want to replace them with your own.

<i>\[Sarah runs her thumb along the insole.\]</i>

<b>SARAH:</b>

They’re lighter than I expected.

<b>PODIATRIST:</b>

And the upper is breathable, so it lets heat escape. That matters when you’re in the same shoes all day.

There’s also a slip-resistant outsole for traction. You still need to follow your hospital’s footwear requirements.

<b>SARAH:</b>

Can I try them?

<b>PODIATRIST:</b>

Absolutely. Walk around. Tell me where you feel pressure.

<i>\[Sarah puts them on and walks across the room.\]</i>

<b>SARAH:<br /><br /></b>Hmm, these are nice. 

My toes aren’t pushed together. My feet feel supported.

<b>PODIATRIST:</b><br />\[Doctor hands her a paper\]

Good. We’ll check the rest of the fit too. 

The insole and upper of the shoes mould to the shape of your feet. So if your feet expands, these shoes also expand.

Shoes are one part of the plan. I also want you to follow these exercises and stretches.<br /><br /><b>SARAH:<br /><br /></b>How much are these? I can’t afford another pair right now. <b><br /><br />PODIATRIST:<br /></b><br />Only \$60 right now since they’re on sale for the holidays. You get 30 days to try them out. They have a great return system in case you’re not happy with them

---

<i>\[Over the following weeks: Sarah getting dressed in her nurse outfit, walking hospital corridors, smiling. Then arriving home.  Wearing her ComfortWear shoes\]</i>

<b>SARAH:<br /></b><br />Hey honey, I’m home

<b>MARK:</b>

How are you feeling, babe? 

<b>SARAH:</b>

Actually… want to go out?

<b>MARK:</b>

Tonight?

<b>SARAH:</b>

Nothing fancy. Somewhere we can walk.

<b>MARK:</b>

Yeah. I’d like that.<br /><br /><b>SARAH:<br /><br /></b>These shoes have really made a difference. I haven’t had any swelling or heel pain. It’s been a few weeks. <br /><br />I don’t want to speak too soon, but i’m really happy you booked the podiatrist for me. Thanks a lot<br /><br />\[SARAH TALKING AND THEN KISSES MARK\]<b><br /><br />MARK:</b>

I’m really happy to hear that. And if you’re up for it, maybe tonight we can finally have fun?<br /><br /><b>SARAH:<br /><br /></b>You bet<br /><br />\[SARAH WINKS AT MARK\] <b> </b>

---

<i>\[They walk together outside. Sarah reaches for his hand.\]</i>

<b>SARAH — VOICEOVER:</b>

I’d got so used to saying, “Maybe on my day off.”

Then spending my day off recovering.

I wanted dinner with my husband. A walk after work. A reason to put something besides scrubs on.

If you’re a nurse who gets home and starts calculating whether you can face one more trip across the room…

Pay attention to that.

Get persistent pain checked. And take a proper look at the shoes carrying you through your shifts.

These ComfortWear shoes have truly changed my life<br /><br />\[Shoe 4 pairs in her shoe area (white blue, black purple, red, black white\]

<b>MARK:</b>

The usual place?

<b>SARAH:</b>

Let’s go a little farther.

## COM339 — Iteration Hokas and Brooks Worst Shoes for Nurses
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Iteration · **Format:** AI + Existing Footage · **Angle:** Nurse · **Persona:** Nurse · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** No Offer · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-20 · **Learnings:** —

For each hook, put a male AI avatar that’s in his late 40s (handsome), wearing a tucked in long sleeve shirt and has a stethoscope around his neck)<br /><br /><i><b>AI AVATAR FOR WHOLE SECTION HERE</b></i><br /><br />Hook 1: OnClouds, Cloves, and New Balance are three of the worst shoes you can wear on a 12-hour shift<br />Hook 2: Danskos, Crocs, and Skech\*rs are three of the worst shoes you can wear on a 12-hour shift<br />Hook 3: As a podiatrist, here is why OnClouds, Hokas, and New Balance are three of the worst shoes you can wear on a 12-hour shift

<br />I know that will upset people. 

It’s not the brand, it’s the design.

These shoes are designed for women looking for something that looks stylish to go for a run, 

not for a nurse that’s going to be working back to back 12s on concrete floor

Here are 3 reasons it can damage your feet<br /><br /><i><b>AI AVATAR ENDS HERE - MASHUP BEGINS</b></i>

<b>#1 - It’s got a triangle-shaped toe box that’s too narrow and doesn’t stretch<br /><br />(HEADLINE: #1 - NARROW TOE-BOX)</b>

On a 12-hour shift, your feet are going to swell and go up an entire size

Your toes are going to jam into the wall, and swell up like sausages by mid-shift

<b>#2 - Onclouds and New Balance were built for tennis players and runners, not to hold a nurse still for 12 hours on concrete.<br /><br />(HEADLINE: #2 - ONCLOUDS & NEW BALANCE MADE FOR RUNNERS & TENNIS)</b>

The thick foam cushion you see is made to spring you during runs, not keep you standing and moving in different directions for 12 hours straight. 

They feel incredible during the first week, but your weight eventually compresses the foam by hour 4

<b>#3 - If you’re in healthcare, you need a slip-resistant grip<br /><br />(HEADLINE #3 - SLIP-RESISTANT GRIP)</b>

OnClouds, New Balance, Hokas, Brooks were built to grip dry pavement, not a floor somebody just mopped 

During your shift in the ER, there’s all kinds of fluids that you can come across, one wet corner on the way to a code and you’re in danger

That's why ComfortWear built the opposite of a running shoe, made for people who stand for a living, not run a marathon.<br /><br />Cushioning that holds all day, a stretch knit upper that widens as your feet swell, <br /><br />a slip-resistant grip made for wet floors <br /><br />and real structural arch support, <br /><br />so plantar fasciitis can't light up your heel by hour 12. <br /><br />More than 500,000 nurses and healthcare workers have already switched. <br /><br />Built for the 12-hour shifts, not marathons. <br /><br />Wear them on your hardest shifts for 30 days. <br /><br />If they don't change how your feet feel, send them back. ## COM340 — Teachers wants to quit drama
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Net New · **Format:** AI · **Angle:** Teacher · **Persona:** Teacher · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** No Offer · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-20 · **Learnings:** —

\[SCENE 1 — TEACHERS’ LOUNGE. Two teachers review a field trip roster. Sarah approaches the open door carrying documents, and overhaering the other teachers near the door\]

<b>JULIE:</b> Take Sarah off the list.

<b>EMMA:</b> Why? It’s her class.

<b>JULIE:</b> Have you seen her after lunch? She barely leaves her chair.

<b>EMMA:</b> Maybe there’s a reason.

<b>JULIE:</b> Well, we need someone who can walk around a museum. Not someone we have to find a bench for because of their foot pain.

\[Sarah steps into the doorway, limping inside the room.\]

<b>SARAH:</b> You could have asked me.

\[Julie looks up. Silence.\]

<b>JULIE:</b> Sarah. We were just sorting out—

<b>SARAH:</b> I heard what you were sorting out.

<b>JULIE:</b> We have thirty children to supervise. We have to be realistic.

<b>SARAH:</b> And you think I don’t know how to supervise my own class?

<b>JULIE:</b> That’s not what I said.

<b>SARAH:</b> You said I’d need a bench.

\[Sarah places the permission slips on the table, but she’s limping to the table. Her hands shake.\]

<b>JULIE:</b> You’ve stayed behind on the last two trips. What were we supposed to think?

<b>SARAH:</b> You could have wondered if I was okay.

\[Sarah leaves, limping. Emma looks at Julie.\]

<b>EMMA:</b> I think you owe her an apology, she just got diagnosed with plantar fasciitis.

\[SCENE 2 — SARAH’S CLASSROOM. Later that afternoon. Sarah sits beside her desk. A student, 8 year old, Sophie, approaches.\]

<b>SOPHIE:</b> Mrs. Harisson? My mom signed it.

<b>SARAH:</b> Thank you, sweetheart. Put it in the tray.

<b>SOPHIE:</b> Are you coming to the museum with us?

<b>SARAH:</b> We’re still working that out.

<b>SOPHIE:</b> I wanted to show you the dinosaurs.

<b>SARAH:</b> You can tell me all about them.

<b>SOPHIE:</b> But it’s better if you’re there.

\[Sarah looks down, composing herself.\]

<b>SARAH:</b> Go get your backpack, okay? Your mom’s waiting.

\[SCENE 3 — EMPTY CLASSROOM. Linda appears at the door. Sarah quickly wipes her face.\]

<b>LINDA:</b> Sarah?

<b>SARAH:</b> I’m fine.

<b>LINDA:</b> I didn’t ask yet.

\[Linda closes the door and sits beside her.\]

<b>SARAH:</b> Julie took me off the museum trip.

<b>LINDA:</b> Did she talk to you first?

<b>SARAH:</b> She’s talking about me to everyone. I overheard her.

<b>LINDA:</b> What did she say?

<b>SARAH:</b> That I barely leave my chair. That they need someone who can keep up with the kids at the museum.

<b>LINDA:</b> Sarah—

<b>SARAH:</b> And I wanted to tell her she was wrong. But look.

\[Sarah gestures toward the chair beside the whiteboard.\]

<b>SARAH:</b> I dragged that over there so I could finish the class without standing. I think the principal is considering firing me.

<b>LINDA:</b> There’s nothing shameful about needing to sit.

<b>SARAH:</b> Then why do I jump up every time someone walks past my door? I’m embarrassed about this.

\[Linda pauses.\]

<b>SARAH:</b> My heels hurt before lunch. By the end of the school day, my shoes feel so tight I take them off in the car. I used to love taking the kids places. Now the first thing I ask is how far we’ll have to walk.

<b>LINDA:</b> Have you had the pain checked?

<b>SARAH:</b> I have. The podiatrist told me I’ve got a bad case of plantar fasciitis and swelling from being on my feet all day. I’m trying to deal with it. But school doesn’t stop while I figure it out.

<b>LINDA:</b> No. It doesn’t.

<b>SARAH:</b> Sophie just asked if I was coming. I couldn’t even give her an answer.

\[SCENE 4 — SAME CLASSROOM. Linda moves her foot into view.\]

<b>LINDA:</b> Can I show you something?

<b>SARAH:</b> If you tell me to buy another expensive pair of sneakers—

<b>LINDA:</b> I’m showing you what I wear. You decide.

<b>SARAH:</b> What are they?

<b>LINDA:</b> ComfortWear. This is their Ortho Stretch Shoe.

<b>SARAH:</b> They look like normal shoes.

<b>LINDA:</b> Look at the front.

\[Linda points to the toe box.\]

<b>LINDA:</b> There’s room here for your toes. And feel this.

\[Sarah gently stretches the knit upper.\]

<b>SARAH:</b> That actually stretches

<b>LINDA:</b> That’s what I wanted across the top of my feet. Something flexible.

<b>SARAH:</b> What about underneath? Soft shoes haven’t always helped me.

<b>LINDA:</b> They’ve got a mouldable insole with arch support and cushioning in the sole.

<b>SARAH:</b> I’ve spent so much money trying to get comfortable. And on top of that, I’ve been wearing these dress shoes to look proper

<b>LINDA:</b> I stopped trying to look proper. In this profession, lasting longer in the career and having a good work life balance is more important for your family

\[Sarah smiles faintly.\]

<b>SARAH:</b> Can you send me the website?

<b>LINDA:</b> Already doing it.

\[SCENE 5 — SARAH’S CLASSROOM. On-screen: “A few weeks later.” Sarah walks between desks as students work. Linda stops at the door.\]

<b>LINDA:</b> How are they feeling?

<b>SARAH:</b> Roomier. More supportive. Especially across the front.

<b>LINDA:</b> And what about during the last recess?

<b>SARAH:</b> More comfortable. I’m not rushing to sit down anymore.

<b>LINDA:</b> I’m glad.

<b>SARAH:</b> I’m still feeling some discomfort here and there

<b>LINDA:</b> That’s normal, give it a few more weeks. Once the shoe moulds to your feet, it will get more comfortable

<b>SARAH:</b> I know. I’m working on believing that.

\[Sarah picks up a clipboard.\]

<b>SARAH:</b> Who has the final museum roster?

\[SCENE 6 — TEACHERS’ LOUNGE. Julie looks up as Sarah enters.\]

<b>SARAH:</b> I’d like my name back on the trip.

<b>JULIE:</b> Are you sure you can—

\[Julie stops herself.\]

<b>JULIE:</b> Sorry. That came out wrong.

<b>SARAH:</b> Yes. It did.

<b>JULIE:</b> Emma told me I owe you an apology.

<b>SARAH:</b> Do you think you do?

\[Pause.\]

<b>JULIE:</b> Yes. I should have asked what was going on.

<b>SARAH:</b> I was in pain, Julie. I wasn’t avoiding my class.

<b>JULIE:</b> I understand.

<b>SARAH:</b> And if I need a break on the trip, I’ll ask for one.

<b>JULIE:</b> Of course.

\[Julie hands her the clipboard. Sarah writes her name.\]

\[SCENE 7 — OUTSIDE THE SCHOOL BUS. Sophie spots Sarah carrying a lunch bag.\]

<b>SOPHIE:</b> Mrs. Harrison! You’re coming!

<b>SARAH:</b> Somebody promised to show me the dinosaurs.

<b>SOPHIE:</b> I know where the big one is!

<b>SARAH:</b> Then you’d better save me a seat.

\[Sophie heads toward the bus. Linda joins Sarah.\]

<b>LINDA:</b> You look happy.

<b>SARAH:</b> I was so embarrassed about that chair, I nearly let someone else decide what kind of teacher I was.

<b>LINDA:</b> Those kids already know what kind of teacher you are.

\[Emma approaches.\]

<b>EMMA:</b> Before you go—Linda said you found some shoes you like that helped with your foot pain?

<b>SARAH:</b> ComfortWear. It’s changed my life.

<b>EMMA:</b> Please. I’ve got playground duty all week, and my feet are already killing me!

<b>SARAH:</b> I’ll send you the website. And look at the size guide before you order.

<b>SOPHIE, FROM THE BUS:</b> Mrs. Sarah! I saved your seat!

\[Sarah turns toward her.\]

<b>SARAH:</b> Coming, sweetheart.

## COM341 — Disney plus-sized woman drama ad
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Net New · **Format:** AI · **Angle:** Elderly Traveling · **Persona:** Travel · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** No Offer · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-20 · **Learnings:** —

\[SCENE 1 — NEAR THE JUNGLE CRUISE ENTRANCE at Disney. Margaret, late 60s, approximately 200 pounds, behind her son Daniel, daughter-in-law Ashley, and grandchildren Lily and Ben. She stops and grips a railing.\]

<b>BEN:</b> Grandma! Come on! We’re doing the boat!

<b>EMILY:</b> I’m coming, sweetheart.

<b>ASHLEY:</b> The line’s getting longer. We need to get in now.

<b>MARGARET:</b> How long is the wait?

<b>DANIEL:</b> About forty minutes.

\[Margaret looks toward the queue.\]

<b>MARGARET:</b> Standing the whole time?

<b>ASHLEY:</b> It’s Disney, Margaret. There are lines.

<b>MARGARET:</b> I know. My heels are just on fire… I need to find a bench for a little while.

<b>ASHLEY:</b> Again?

<b>DANIEL:</b> Ashley.

<b>ASHLEY:</b> What? We stopped on the way in. We stopped after breakfast. Now we’re stopping before we’ve even done this ride.

<b>MARGARET:</b> You go. I’ll wait.

<b>LILY:</b> But Grandma’s supposed to sit with me.

<b>ASHLEY:</b> Then Grandma should have thought about how much walking this would involve.

\[Margaret looks down.\]

<b>DANIEL:</b> That’s enough.

<b>ASHLEY:</b> We planned this for months. If she couldn’t manage it, maybe she shouldn’t have come.

\[Silence. Ben stops pulling on Margaret’s hand.\]

<b>MARGARET:</b> I didn’t know it would be this bad.

<b>ASHLEY:</b> I’m just saying what everyone’s thinking.

<b>MARGARET:</b> Are you?

\[She looks at Daniel. He hesitates.\]

<b>DANIEL:</b> Mom, let me find somewhere for you—

<b>MARGARET:</b> No. Take the children.

<b>LILY:</b> Grandma—

<b>MARGARET:</b> Tell me if you see a crocodile, okay?

\[Margaret turns away before Lily can see her cry.\]

\[SCENE 2 — A NEARBY WALKWAY. Margaret moves slowly toward a bench. Another older woman, Ruth, notices her stop.\]

<b>RUTH:</b> Would an arm help?

<b>MARGARET:</b> Oh, I don’t want to hold you up.

<b>RUTH:</b> I asked if you wanted an arm, dear. Not an apology.

\[Margaret takes her arm. They reach the bench.\]

<b>RUTH:</b> There we are. Take your time.

<b>MARGARET:</b> Thank you.

<b>RUTH:</b> Family on a ride?

<b>MARGARET:</b> Jungle Cruise.

<b>RUTH:</b> Mine too.

<b>MARGARET:</b> You couldn’t do the line either?

<b>RUTH:</b> I did it this morning. My grandson wanted another turn. I wanted an ice cream.

\[Margaret gives a small laugh.\]

<b>RUTH:</b> Your feet?

<b>MARGARET:</b> My heels. Then everything starts aching because I’m trying not to put weight on them.

<b>RUTH:</b> I’ve had trouble with heel pain myself.

<b>MARGARET:</b> It’s humiliating.

<b>RUTH:</b> The pain?

<b>MARGARET:</b> Having everyone wait. Watching them pretend they aren’t annoyed.

\[Her voice breaks.\]

<b>MARGARET:</b> Although today nobody bothered pretending.

<b>RUTH:</b> What happened?

<b>MARGARET:</b> My daughter-in-law said I shouldn’t have come.

<b>RUTH:</b> Oh, sweetheart.

<b>MARGARET:</b> I’m two hundred pounds. I know that. I see myself in the pictures. I don’t need everyone looking at me like I’ve done this on purpose.

<b>RUTH:</b> You came to spend time with your grandchildren.

<b>MARGARET:</b> And now they’re making memories while I’m sitting here on the bench.

<b>RUTH:</b> You deserve to be in those memories, too.

\[SCENE 3 — ON THE BENCH. Ruth gestures toward her shoes, worn out walmart shoes.\]

<b>RUTH:</b> Have you found a pair that feels comfortable for longer walks?

<b>MARGARET:</b> I thought I had. They felt fine at Walmart

<b>RUTH:</b> Mine used to pass the shop test, too.

<b>MARGARET:</b> What are those?

<b>RUTH:</b> ComfortWear. The Ortho Stretch Shoes.

<b>MARGARET:</b> How do they feel? They look comfy, but they’re all comfy until you’re walking 8 hours

<b>RUTH:</b> These are the ones I packed.

\[She points to the front.\]

<b>RUTH:</b> See how much room there is around the toes?

<b>MARGARET:</b> That’s where mine feel tight right now.

<b>RUTH:</b> And the fabric has some stretch across the top in case my feet swell.

\[She gently stretches the knit.\]

<b>MARGARET:</b> What about support? I don’t want something that just feels like a slipper.

<b>RUTH:</b> They have an insole with arch support and cushioning underneath. And they’re lightweight.

<b>MARGARET:</b> How long have you had them?

<b>RUTH:</b> About three years. I usually get a fresh pair each year. Sometimes another color.

<b>MARGARET:</b> And you’re happy with them?

<b>RUTH:</b> I have only been wearing them for the last 3 years. For running errands, going for dinner, lunch with my girls, and even pickleball

<b>MARGARET:</b> Could you write down the name?

## COM346 — I just got back from Barcelona
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Net New · **Format:** AI + Existing Footage · **Angle:** Travel · **Persona:** Travel · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** No Offer · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-20 · **Learnings:** —

Hook 1: I just got back from 2 years in Barcelona, and everyone keeps asking me how I’m walking so much and how I went from 250lbs to 180lbs<br />Hook 2: I tried the Hokas, Skechers, and Brooks in Barcelona, but none of them helped my foot pain

Hook 3: I asked plus-sized women who traveled to Barcelona what shoes they wore on their trip. The answer was always the same.  

I’ll tell you exactly what changed

My husband and I moved there when I was 62

Within the first week, I noticed something that honestly made me feel terrible about myself.

Women my age were walking everywhere.

Across cobblestones. Up hills. Carrying groceries back to their apartments.

Then going out again after dinner.

Meanwhile, I was checking whether the restaurant had somewhere to sit outside before agreeing to leave.

I’m a plus-sized woman. For years, I’d told myself that sore feet were just part of carrying a bigger body.

Then I met our neighbor, Elena.

She was 66.

She told me she used to weigh 300 lbs, but after moving to Barcelona and changing her shoes, she lost a lot of weight

She’d walk to the market, come home with two bags, and ask if I wanted to go for coffee.

I was still recovering from the market.

Same cobblestones. Same hills. Around the same age.

But she wasn’t planning her entire afternoon around taking her shoes off.

I told myself she was used to it.

Of course she could walk farther. She’d lived there for years.

But after a few weeks, I noticed something.

She kept reaching for the same pair of shoes.

Meanwhile, I’d gone through 3 different popular brands

<b>First, Hoka Bondi, 6/10</b>

All that cushioning looked like exactly what I needed. Soft when I tried them on. Comfortable walking around the house.

But walking around my kitchen wasn’t the same as spending an afternoon on Barcelona’s cobblestones.

By the afternoon, the foam insole had gone flat, and my toes felt squeezed from the narrow toe-box

<b>Then Skechers, 5/10</b>

Lightweight. Easy to slip on. That memory foam felt lovely at first.

But after hours of sightseeing and exploring, my feet felt like they were walking on cardboard. There’s no arch support, barely any cushioning, and the toe-box is also narrow.

<b>Then Brooks, 7/10</b>

I thought, these are serious walking-and-running shoes. Surely this pair would do it.

But by lunchtime, the front of mine felt crowded. I was loosening the laces, trying to give my feet more room.

Three different popular brands.

Same conversation with my husband.

“You go ahead. I’ll sit here.”

One afternoon, Elena and I were having coffee, and I finally asked her.

“How do you do it? You’ve already walked to La Boqueria market, Sagrada Familia and back. I’m sitting here wondering whether I can manage the walk home.”

She looked down at my shoes.

Then she said something I still remember.

“You keep telling me how comfortable they were when you bought them. How do they feel after you’ve been out all day?”

That was exactly it.

I was buying shoes for the first five minutes.

Barcelona was everything that happened after that.

Cobblestones. Stone steps. Standing at the market. Walking to lunch. Then walking back.

What felt comfortable at breakfast could feel completely different by three.

And I kept thinking I just needed something softer.

Elena pointed to her shoes.

ComfortWear.

“These are the ones I keep coming back to.”

I ordered a pair that night.

Supportive walking shoes with a wide toe box and a soft, stretchy upper.

The whole design comes down to one idea.

Your shoes should give your feet room and support for the hours you spend in them.

The insole has built-in arch support, so there’s support underneath the middle of your foot, with cushioning at the heel.

For those long afternoons walking across cobblestones, that was the combination I’d been looking for.

The toe box is wide, so your toes have room to spread instead of being squeezed together at the front.

When your feet feel fuller by the afternoon, you appreciate having that space.

The upper is stretchy knit, so it gives you room across the top of your foot instead of holding it inside a stiff shell.

Less of that trapped feeling I kept getting in my other shoes.

The outsole is slip-resistant, with a strong grip.

Something I’d learned to pay attention to on Barcelona’s old cobblestone streets, especially after rain.

And the shoe is lightweight, so it doesn’t feel like something bulky you have to carry through another afternoon of sightseeing.

Support underneath. Room at the front. Stretchy material across the top.

Shoes I wanted to keep walking in.

I started wearing them on my everyday walks.

At first, just coffee with Elena. Then the market. Then a few streets farther before heading home.

Gradually, I spent less of those walks thinking about my feet.

One afternoon, Elena stopped me on the way back from lunch.

“You haven’t asked me to find a bench.”

I laughed.

She was right.

Then she asked if I wanted to take the longer way home.

And I said yes.

That was how it happened.

Short walks became longer walks.

My husband and I started going out after dinner. I walked to pick up groceries. Sometimes, I went out because there was a street I hadn’t explored yet.

Over the following months, I lost some weight too, as walking became a much bigger part of my routine.

I’m still a plus-sized woman.

But I’m spending more of my day doing the things I used to talk myself out of.

So if you’re planning a trip and already wondering whether you’ll keep up, think about what your feet will need after hours of sightseeing.

Room for your toes.

Support under your arches.

An upper that stretches when your feet feel fuller later in the day

That’s what I appreciated about ComfortWear.

I’ll link them below. Their Black Friday sale is on now, with 60% off sitewide.

Try them before your trip. See how they feel on your own feet.

There were so many little streets in Barcelona I almost never walked down.

I’m glad I gave myself the chance.

## COM352 — 3 reasons why podiatrists recommend these shoes
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Net New · **Format:** AI + Existing Footage · **Angle:** Plus-sized · **Persona:** Expert / Doctor · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** No Offer · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-21 · **Learnings:** —

Hook 1: 3 reasons plus-sized women need to stop wearing Hokas & Skech\*rs (PUT AI MALE DOCTOR WITH LAB COAT SITTING DOWN)<br /><br />Hook 2: After analyzing the top 5 shoes women for over 250lbs are wearing, here are 3 reasons why they need to change the shoes they’ve been wearing (PUT AI MALE DOCTOR WITH LAB COAT IN GREEN SCREEN WITH SKECHERS AND HOKAS ON PLUS-SIZED WOMAN IN THE BACKRGROUND)

Hook 3: 3 reasons why podiatrists love these shoes for plus-sized women (PUT AI MALE DOCTOR WITH LAB COAT SITTING DOWN WHILE HOLDING COMFORTWEAR SHOES)

<br />Reason #1, soft foam goes flat faster under more weight

Memory foam feels amazing in the store for the first 5 minutes, but every step you take while walking presses it down. For a plus-sized woman, the more load that gets put on the insole, the flatter it gets by midday. By the evening, you’re walking on cardboard

Reason #2, your feet are wider and swell more throughout the day

Most sneakers are narrow in front and stiff on top. By the afternoon your toes are pressed together and the laces are digging in. You want a wide toe box and a top that gives as your feet swell.

Reason #3, taller heel isn’t better

Those big, tall, cushioned shoes are soft and wobbly underneath, so under more weight your foot tilts in with every step, and by the afternoon your ankles and knees feel it.

So that's the checklist: 

#1 firm arch support

#2 wide toe-box to let your toes spread naturally 

#3 lighter and steadier. 

Most shoes give you one of the three. At our clinic, this is the shoe we've been recommending: ComfortWear. It's the first one I've seen that does all three

One: the arch underneath is firm, not squishy. Press it and it springs right back.

Two: a wide toe box and a knit top that actually stretches as your feet swell

Three: it's lightweight, with a sole that grips instead of rocking.

Listen to what this customer had to say (PUT SCENE OF DOCTOR SITTING DOWN AND THE TESTIMONIAL OF [customer] ON THE TOP RIGHT while the doctor is listening)

And they're under \$60 right now. You get 30 days to try them. If they don't feel different by the end of your day, send them back.

## COM358 — We asked 500 women 72+ years old at Disney
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Iteration · **Format:** UGC · **Angle:** Disney Senior · **Persona:** Senior +65 · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** 40% Off · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-21 · **Learnings:** —

Reference ad: [https://fb.me/adspreview/facebook/23rM9Sszj7AU0PD](https://fb.me/adspreview/facebook/23rM9Sszj7AU0PD)

Adapt this structure to the elderly at Disney target

---

Hook 1:

We asked 500 women aged 72 and older at Disney one simple question:

What are you looking for when buying shoes?

Hook 2:

If you’re visiting Disney in your 70s, stop taking shoe advice from younger people.

We asked 500 women aged 72 and older at Disney what kind of shoes they prefer.

Hook 3:

If you’re visiting Disney in your 70s, the wrong shoes can make a day with your family feel twice as long.

We interviewed 500 women aged 72 and older about the shoes they wear to the parks.

Body:

The same things kept coming up.

Comfort. Support. And shoes they could wear through a full day at the parks.

Because walking to the first attraction is one thing.

But walking from ride to ride, standing in long lines, and crossing the park again for the fireworks is something else.

One woman told us her heels were already aching before lunch.

Another said her shoes felt fine at the hotel. By the afternoon, they were squeezing the front of her feet.

And another said the hardest part was having to sit out while her grandchildren went on to the next attraction.

So “comfortable when you first put them on” isn’t going to cut it.

The real question is: how do your feet feel when there are still three hours left in the park?

First, Skechers.

A familiar name. Plenty of styles. And an easy place to start when you’re looking for comfortable walking shoes.

But the difficulty some women describe shows up later.

One woman told us hers felt soft at first. After hours of walking and standing in lines, her heels were aching.

Another said the cushioning wore down before the rest of the shoe did.

You need that comfort to hold up beyond the walk from the hotel.

Next, HOKA.

The thick cushioning makes them an obvious option when your feet already hurt.

But more cushioning doesn’t guarantee you’ve found the right shoe for a full day at Disney.

One woman told us the bulky sole made her feel wobbly and unsure of her footing, especially on the cobblestone paths at EPCOT.

Another said she worried about rolling her ankle every time she stepped onto uneven ground.

That thick sole might feel like a cloud at first.

But when you’re carefully watching every step instead of enjoying what’s around you, softness isn’t enough.

You want to feel comfortable. And confident on your feet.

Then there’s New Balance.

Another name that comes up, with plenty of people who like them.

But some women are still adding something before those shoes feel right.

Extra insoles. More heel padding. Another adjustment to make room around a sensitive spot.

One woman told us she bought hers for the support, then bought separate inserts because her heels still hurt.

Another said she loosened the laces throughout the day because the front started feeling too tight.

That’s more money, more adjusting, and another thing to deal with when you’re supposed to be enjoying your trip.

So, what actually works?

After looking through hundreds of reviews on Reddit and talking to women aged 72 and older who spend full days walking around Disney, the same answer kept coming up.

ComfortWear.

The pair women said they could still comfortably wear when it was time for the fireworks.

A roomy toe box gives your toes space instead of squeezing them together, especially when your feet feel swollen after a long day.

A stretch upper flexes across the foot for a more adaptable fit.

Plus, built-in arch support and heel cushioning bring support and softness together underfoot.

Many popular athletic shoes are designed with running or exercise in mind.

ComfortWear brings the focus back to comfort for long days on your feet.

Because when you leave the park, the day isn’t always over.

There’s the walk back to the hotel.

Dinner with the family.

And another day of memories waiting tomorrow.

Right now, ComfortWear is 40% off with free shipping, and you get 30 days to try them — more than enough time to see how they feel before your trip.

Your need for comfort doesn’t end when you leave the park.

Tap below to find your fit and give ComfortWear a try.

Hook 2:

If you’re going through menopause, stop taking shoe advice from 30-year-olds.

We asked 500 women going through menopause what kind of shoes they prefer.

Hook 3:

If you’re going through menopause, the wrong shoes can make an ordinary day on your feet feel twice as long.

We interviewed 500 women about the shoes they were wearing—and what was still hurting.

Body:

The same things kept coming up.

Shoes that suddenly felt too tight.

Heels that hurt with those first steps in the morning.

And knees that felt stiff after getting up from a chair.

One woman told us she was still buying the same size, from the same brand.

But now, the front of the shoe pressed against her toes.

Another said she used to walk every morning.

Now, she found herself thinking about her heels before she even got out of bed.

And another said she was tired of choosing between shoes that squeezed her feet and shoes that felt too bulky.

Because everyone talks about hot flashes.

Fewer people talk about what this stage of life can feel like when you’re simply trying to get through the day on your feet.

Here’s the simple explanation.

As estrogen levels fall during the menopause transition, joint aches and stiffness can become more common.

The muscles, tendons, and ligaments that help you move can be affected, too.

So familiar activities—walking, climbing stairs, standing in the kitchen—may start feeling less comfortable.

Changes in weight can also add load to your feet and knees.

And foot shape can change over time, which means the size and fit you’ve always bought may no longer feel right.

That sharp heel pain some women describe?

One possible cause is plantar fasciitis.

That’s when the band of tissue supporting the bottom of your foot becomes irritated, often making those first steps after resting especially painful.

So “these have always been my shoes” isn’t going to cut it.

The real question is: do they still work for the feet you have now?

First, Skechers.

A familiar name. Plenty of styles. And that soft feeling that makes them easy to like in the store.

But the difficulty some women describe shows up later.

One woman told us hers felt comfortable for a quick errand.

After a longer afternoon on her feet, her heels were aching again.

Another said the soft insole was the reason she bought them—but it didn’t solve the pressure around her toes.

That’s the limitation.

Softness underneath doesn’t fix a fit that squeezes across the front.

And a comfortable first impression doesn’t tell you how your feet will feel three hours later.

Next, HOKA.

The thick cushioning makes them an obvious option when your heels already hurt.

But more cushioning doesn’t solve every complaint.

One woman told us she liked the softness under her heel, but found the shoe bulky for everyday wear.

Another said the front still felt restrictive, even though the sole felt cushioned.

You can have plenty of foam underneath and still feel pressure where you need more space.

And if you don’t enjoy walking in the shoe, that thick sole isn’t enough.

Then there’s New Balance.

Another name women turn to when they’re looking for support.

But some were still making adjustments after buying them.

Extra inserts.

Looser laces.

Trying another size because the first pair felt too tight across the front.

One woman told us she bought hers for support, then added separate insoles because her heels still felt uncomfortable.

Another said sizing up gave her toes more room—but left the heel feeling too loose.

That’s more money. More trial and error.

And another pair of shoes that almost works.

So, what actually works?

After talking to women going through menopause about what they were wearing—and what they were still missing—the same answer kept coming up.

ComfortWear.

The pair that brought the features they were looking for together.

Starting with a roomy toe box.

Space for your toes to sit comfortably, instead of being pressed together at the front.

Especially helpful when a narrow fit has become the first thing you notice about your shoes.

A stretch upper flexes across the foot for a more adaptable fit.

So you’re not relying on a rigid upper to accommodate a foot that feels different from one part of the day to the next.

Plus, built-in arch support and heel cushioning bring support and softness together underfoot.

Because cushioning alone wasn’t the whole answer.

Neither was buying a bigger size.

These women were looking for room across the front, comfort under the heel, and support they could wear through their daily routine.

That’s what makes ComfortWear our pick.

Shoes won’t change your hormones.

But the right fit can make a difference to how comfortable you feel in them.

For your morning walk.

An afternoon of errands.

Or standing in the kitchen after a day that’s already asked a lot of you.

You’re already adjusting to enough changes.

Your shoes shouldn’t be another thing you have to put up with.

Right now, ComfortWear is 40% off with free shipping and a 30-day money-back guarantee.

Tap below to find your fit and give ComfortWear a try.

## COM364 — Doctor rating shoes at Disney
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Iteration · **Format:** AI · **Angle:** Disney · **Persona:** Travel · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** — · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-21 · **Learnings:** —

INSTRUCTIONS: Following the [INT_05.28.26_Nurse Page_[creator]390v1_H5](https://fb.me/adspreview/facebook/1XSjAbCxX2rzH2k) winning ad’s structure, we’ll create a version where the doctor is set at a Disney medical office. He won’t be ranking shoes for nurses, but shoes for spending a full day at the Disney parks.

> 👾 **Avatar generation:**
>
> Create a photorealistic vertical 9:16 talking-head video featuring a fictional male medical presenter, approximately 55–60 years old, with neatly styled salt-and-pepper hair, a white lab coat, a light blue shirt, and a calm, approachable expression. He sits behind a warm wooden desk, facing the camera, hands naturally visible.
>
> The setting is a private office overlooking a Disney theme park. Through a large window behind him, show a recognizable fairytale castle, landscaped gardens, and softly blurred park visitors. The park must remain clearly visible without distracting from the presenter.
>
> Inside the office, include tasteful park memorabilia: Mickey ears on a shelf, a pin-trading lanyard, and a framed vacation photograph. Add a small anatomical foot model and one subtle foot anatomy print to connect with the reference image.
>
> Use soft natural daylight, realistic skin texture, accurate hands, and restrained conversational gestures. Frame from the waist up, leaving space for shoe overlays and large captions.

---

────────────────────
OPENER- FULL-SCREEN AVATAR
────────────────────

**HOOK 1**

SUPER AT THE TOP: **DOCTOR RECOMMENDS DISNEY PARK SHOES FOR PLUS-SIZE WOMEN**

If you’re over 200lbs, choosing shoes for a full Disney park day can take a little more thought.

**HOOK 2**

SUPER AT THE TOP: **DOCTOR RECOMMENDS DISNEY PARK SHOES FOR WOMEN 65+**

If you’re over 60, choosing shoes for a full Disney park day can take a little more thought.

**HOOK 3**

SUPER AT THE TOP: **DOCTOR RECOMMENDS SHOES FOR PEOPLE WITH PLANTAR FASCIITIS AT DISNEY**

If you have plantar fasciitis, choosing shoes for a full Disney park day can take a little more thought.

You’re walking between attractions. Standing in long lines. Crossing the park again because someone wants one more ride. And by the fireworks, your feet have been working all day.

Let’s talk about three popular choices and what to consider before spending an entire park day in them.

────────────────────
HOKA — SPLIT SCREEN
────────────────────

TOP: Avatar continues speaking.
BOTTOM: Actual HOKA model being discussed. Close-ups of the sole and heel fit, followed by walking footage on cobblestone-style paving in EPCOT.

“First, HOKA.

That chunky, cushioned sole can feel great when you first put it on.

But a tall sole doesn’t automatically give you a steady feel.

I’ve had patients come to my office after rolling an ankle, saying they felt wobbly when walking on those EPCOT cobblestone streets.

Plus, they’re on the heavier side, so that’s something to consider when you’re wearing them for 14 hours straight.”

────────────────────
CROCS CLASSIC — SPLIT SCREEN
────────────────────

TOP: Avatar speaking.
BOTTOM: Crocs Classic being put on with the heel strap engaged. Show a close-up of the fit, then normal walking footage in the park.

“Next, Crocs Classic.

Easy to slip on. Plenty of room around the toes.

But roominess can become a drawback if your foot slides around inside the shoe.

Imagine picking up the pace to catch your family before the next attraction. If your foot keeps shifting and sliding with every step, it can create unnecessary friction on your soles and the sides of your toes.

They also lack arch support, and that can create extra pressure on your soles.”

────────────────────
SKECHERS GOWALK — SPLIT SCREEN
────────────────────

TOP: Avatar speaking.
BOTTOM: The specific GoWalk version being discussed. Show walking, standing in an attraction queue, and close-ups of the heel and toe fit.

“Third, Skechers GoWalk.

That memory foam sole can make a great first impression. The shoes feel great when you try them in the store. I’ve tried them myself!

But I’ve seen several people wearing them shift their weight by hour five because the foam has gotten flat. And they still had a full day ahead.

Don’t judge the pair only by how soft it feels when you first slip it on. Give it a longer trial during your everyday routine, with time spent both walking and standing.”

────────────────────
PAIN POINTS — FULL-SCREEN AVATAR
────────────────────

“If you’re carrying extra weight, give yourself time to find a fit that feels comfortable and secure.

If you’re older, pay attention to how steady you feel when walking and turning.

And if you have plantar fasciitis, don’t make your first park day the first real test of your shoes.

Your feet deserve some planning before the vacation starts.”

────────────────────
COMFORTWEAR REVEAL — FULL-SCREEN AVATAR
────────────────────

“A long park day puts several things to the test at once.

Room for your toes. A secure fit. Support underfoot. And traction for the surfaces along the way.

Those are the details we focused on with ComfortWear.”

────────────────────
COMFORTWEAR FEATURES — SPLIT SCREEN
────────────────────

TOP: Avatar speaking.
BOTTOM: Real ComfortWear product footage. Match each feature to its corresponding detail shot.

“Start with the wide toe box.

Your toes have room without feeling crowded together at the front of the shoe.

That matters when you’re spending hours on your feet and a snug fit starts feeling more restrictive.

You shouldn’t have to curl your toes or squeeze them together just to fit into your shoes.”

“Then there’s the flexible upper.

It accommodates changes in fit as your feet swell throughout the day, with material that gives instead of feeling rigid across the top of your foot.

And the lace-free design means no laces cinching down over that area.

So when you’re halfway through the day, you have room to work with.”

“Underneath, contoured arch support gives your foot a supported feel.

Because a day at Disney includes more than walking.

It includes standing through a long attraction line, waiting for the parade, and getting back on your feet after a quick break.”

────────────────────
EMOTIONAL PAYOFF — FULL-SCREEN FOOTAGE
────────────────────

Real footage of a family enjoying the park near sunset, stopping for a photo, and watching the fireworks. Avatar continues in voiceover.

“You planned the castle photo.

The ride everyone’s been talking about.

The fireworks at the end of the night.

When you’re choosing your shoes, think about all of it, including the walk back.”

“And the textured grip sole is designed to provide traction as you move across different surfaces.

From paved paths to textured walkways, traction is another part of choosing shoes for the day ahead.”

────────────────────
CTA — FULL-SCREEN AVATAR
────────────────────

ComfortWear shoe on the desk. Avatar looks directly into the camera.

“Try ComfortWear with our 30-day trial, plus free shipping.

Wear them during your everyday routine before your trip, and see how they feel on your feet. If you don’t like them, you can get your money back. No questions asked.”

## COM365 — Long format text_DisneyOverweight
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Iteration · **Format:** UGC · **Angle:** Plus size women Disney · **Persona:** Overweight · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** — · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-21 · **Learnings:** —

REFERENCE AD: [https://fb.me/adspreview/facebook/2cgjxWuhZcpWtjS](https://fb.me/adspreview/facebook/2cgjxWuhZcpWtjS)

_Instructions:_

Split the copy into 3 frames, with 2 text blocks per frame:

1. Plus-size callout + problem.
2. Brand comparison + stronger closing line.
3. Comfortwear + benefits + offer and CTA.

Use real-life UGC B-roll of plus-size women at Disney: walking around the parks, waiting in line, and taking breaks. Prioritize natural, phone-shot footage. Keep text large and high-contrast, and hold each frame long enough to read comfortably.

---

**FRAME 1**

**Hook 1:** If you’re a plus-size woman planning a Disney trip, your shoes deserve as much thought as your hotel.

That pair that feels so soft at home? Hours of walking and standing in line can tell a different story. Your feet start swelling, your toes feel squeezed, and you still have half the park left before the fireworks.

**Hook 2:** If you’re a plus-size woman heading to Disney, will your shoes still have room for your feet after six hours of walking in the Florida heat?

**Hook 3:** If you’re a plus-size woman planning a Disney trip, the last thing you want is to send your group ahead to the next ride because you need another break.

**FRAME 2**

Skechers for the memory foam. Hokas for the thick cushioning. Nike Air Max because they’re familiar. On Clouds because they feel so light. It’s easy to see why they make the shortlist.

But soft foam, thick soles, and a lightweight feel mean nothing if your toes feel trapped and you’re desperate to take your shoes off by lunch.

**FRAME 3**

That’s where Comfortwear comes in. A roomy toe box gives your toes space, a stretchy upper accommodates your feet as the day goes on, and supportive cushioning adds comfort without a bulky feel.

Right now, Comfortwear is 40% off with free shipping, plus 30 days to try them. Give them a full day on your feet before your trip. They might become the most important thing you pack for Disney.

_Body_

‼️ ***Even though the structure is almost the same as the original ad, make sure to replace some footage throughout the body of the ad so it’s not the exact same.***

First up, On Cloud, around $160. Seems like the obvious choice, right? Comfortable at home, look decent enough for dinner out.

The problem is On built these for running tracks and gym floors, not 400-year-old cobblestones. The toe box is narrow, fine for a 45-minute run, brutal for a full day of sightseeing.

By hour five, both of our testers were looking for somewhere to sit. Their feet didn't feel tired, they felt beaten up.

Next, we tried Hokas, the big ones, around $165. For the first two hours, honestly, they felt great.

But here's how Hokas fool you. All that thick cushioning is built to absorb impact going straight down, like when you're running on a flat road.

On cobblestones, your foot rolls side to side with every single step, and all that foam gives you almost nothing for that. You're actually less stable than you'd be in a flatter shoe.

On top of that, Hokas are heavy, almost twice the weight of a normal shoe. By hour seven, you're not just fighting tired feet. Your legs are carrying that extra weight with every step you take.

Our testers said their knees hurt more than their feet by the end of the day.

Then we tried New Balance, about $150.

Soft cushioning, a structured upper, and a design built for everyday runs. It’s easy to see why you’d pack them

But cushioning and stability aren’t the same thing. This is a neutral running shoe. If you need extra stability, that soft feeling alone doesn’t mean you’re getting it.

And on a trip packed with cobblestones, old staircases, and hours on your feet, softness shouldn’t be your only selling point.

By early afternoon, right when you're heading into your second museum, or climbing up to that view you flew nine hours to see, you have the least support of the entire day.

Then we tested Comfortwear, and this is where the tests stopped being a competition.

Comfortwear doesn't make shoes for athletes. They make shoes for people whose feet have to survive real life. Nurses, healthcare workers, and travelers who want to actually enjoy the trip they've been saving for.

The sole is firm enough to protect you from cobblestones, but flexible enough that you're not clomping around like you're wearing hiking boots.

The toe box is wide. Your toes can actually spread out and do their job, instead of getting crushed together after mile three.

And the arch support doesn't quit at 2 p.m. It's still there at hour 9, when you're walking back from dinner along the river.

After a full day of testing, 22,000 steps through an old city center, our testers all said the same thing. Their feet didn't hurt. Not hurt less, didn't hurt.

That means you're not the person cutting the day short because your feet gave out. You're not sitting on a bench watching other people see what you flew there to see. You're still going.

Right now, Comfortwear is 40% off with free shipping, and you get 45 days to try them. More than enough time to put them through a real trip.

If they don't hold up better than anything else you've ever packed, send them back.

But based on everything we tested, you'll never pack anything else.

## COM369 — I owe every patient an apology
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Net New · **Format:** UGC · **Angle:** Overweight · **Persona:** Overweight · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** — · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-21 · **Learnings:** —

REFERENCE AD: [app.tryatria.com](https://app.tryatria.com/ad/m994577819689767)

> 📽️ Please create this ad just using UGC/real life people Broll, no AI. Search for 50-60 year old doctor’s in their offices and plus sized women.
>
> Prioritize showing overweight women struggling in the opener

_Hook 1:_

‘You don’t have a foot problem darling… it’s a weight problem.’

_Hook 2:_

‘Come back and see me when you lose 30 pounds’

_Hook 3:_

“At this weight, your knees are doing the work of two people.”

_Shared body:_

I used to dismiss patients with lines like that. And I owe them a massive apology.

The ones who came to my office with aching feet. Sore knees. A back that hurt after a simple trip to the grocery store.

And heard the same answer:

“You need to lose weight.”

I should have looked further.

Because when I made their weight the whole conversation, I missed a question that deserved an answer:

“What are you wearing on your feet?”

You’re told to walk more.

But walking is what hurts.

So you push through. Cut the walk short. Find somewhere to sit.

And eventually, you start thinking your body is the problem.

Weight can contribute to pain. But that doesn’t mean it explains everything.

And it certainly doesn’t mean you have to reach a number on the scale before your comfort deserves attention.

Here’s the part I should have paid more attention to:

The shoes.

A shoe can look comfortable and still be the wrong fit for your feet.

Take Hoka. You see that thick sole and think, “Finally. Enough cushioning.”
But if the pair you’re wearing feels too bulky or unsteady when you turn, more foam isn’t answering what you need. It might cause more trouble if your steps don’t feel steady on the ground, increasing the risk of a twisted ankle.

Then there’s New Balance. They have wider-than-average styles, which might seem like a solution.

But many styles lack structural arch support, which means your feet have to work harder to support your body weight. This can cause overpronation over time, so your foot arches flatten and roll too far inward with every step, straining the muscles and tendons.

Skechers is another brand that I see a lot of people pick. They are affordable and have a memory foam sole, which seems like a great option.

But that marshmallow-like foam tends to wear down quickly, and it ends up packing down into a thin, dense, hardened layer. Once flattened, all shock absorption is lost, which can lead to heel pain, fatigue in your calves, and even strain on your knees.

So instead of starting with a brand name, let’s start with what your feet need.

When you’re carrying more weight, there’s more load on your feet. That makes the details worth looking at.

You need room across the front, so your toes aren’t squeezed.

Cushioning underfoot, to soften the feeling of each step.

Firm, protective support through the arch.

And an upper that accommodates your foot comfortably, rather than pressing into it.

Those are the features I want you to look for.

And that brings me to ComfortWear.

Their Ortho Stretch model solves all of these problems at once.

The wide toe box finally gives your toes room to breathe and splay naturally.

The built-in arch support adds a resilient foundation beneath your feet that doesn't collapse under load, preventing overpronation.

The adaptive cushioning helps make everyday steps feel softer on hard tile or asphalt.

And the stretchable, premium upper completely accommodates your foot’s unique shape without friction or digging into your skin.

When you finally give your body the right foundation, the timeline of how your body recovers is remarkable.

By day three of wearing them, that deep, throbbing ache you usually feel in your heels right after waking up starts to quiet down. Your feet stop burning by the afternoon because the muscles aren't working overtime just to keep you upright.

After one week, the benefits travel up your kinetic chain. Because your ankles are no longer rolling inward, your knees stop taking a pounding, and that tightness in your calves begins to fade.

By week two, you’ll realize something incredible: you’re standing at the kitchen counter making dinner, or walking through the grocery store, and you haven't looked for a place to sit once. Your lower back isn't locked up. You aren't cutting your day short. You simply get your energy, and your life, back.

The goal isn’t to train for a marathon. It’s to find a pair that fits your feet and makes those everyday moments more comfortable.

So here’s what I wish I’d said sooner:

“You deserve to be heard. And we should look at the whole picture.”

Including what’s under your feet.

Before you buy another pair based on a trendy logo, take a closer look at ComfortWear. Right now they’re running a sale and everything’s 40% off, with free shipping. Plus, you get a 30-day money-back guarantee to try them on at home, walk around your living room, and see how they feel risk-free.

Click below, explore the fit and features, and find your next everyday pair.

## COM370 — I have spinal stenosis
**Status:** — · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-21 · **Learnings:** —

_(No script — page holds only research notes:)_

- Alter your gait: Without proper cushioning or alignment, your walking pattern changes, leading to uneven muscle use and stress on the lower back.
- Disrupt pelvic alignment: Shoes that tilt your body forward or fail to stabilize your feet can cause your pelvis to rotate or tilt, putting pressure on the lumbar spine.
- Increase impact forces: Each step sends shock waves upward through your body. Without adequate shock absorption, these forces travel directly to your spine, potentially aggravating existing back issues.
- Encourage compensatory posture changes: If your feet hurt or feel unstable, you may hunch your shoulders, lean to one side, or lock your knees. These are all movements that can trigger pain in the upper and lower back.

Next, Hokas, around $165. For the first two hours, honestly, they felt great. But here's how Hokas fool you: all that cushion is built for running straight ahead. On the stairs up to the pedestrian bridges and the slopes between resorts, your foot rolls with every step. And they're heavy, almost twice a normal shoe. By hour seven, our testers said their knees hurt more than their feet.

Then Brooks, $140. Sounds sensible. But here's the mistake: comfortable walking doesn't mean comfortable standing. Vegas is standing: in line for the buffet, in line for the show, in line for a taxi at midnight. The line doesn't get shorter when your feet start aching

Skechers, $90. Affordable, and that memory foam feels amazing when you first put them on. But that's the problem nobody thinks about before Vegas: memory foam goes flat under your weight. By the time you're walking back from the fountains, you're on flat rubber with zero support

Then we tested ComfortWear, $60

A wide toe box, so your toes have space instead of being crowded together.

Soft, stretchy knit that gives you a more forgiving fit.

Built-in arch support for those long stretches on your feet.

The sole is firm enough for hot pavement and miles of casino floor

After a full Strip day, 22,000 steps from one end to the other, our testers all said the same thing: their feet didn't hurt. Not hurt less. Didn't hurt at all and still felt comfortable by the time they got back to the hotel.

Right now ComfortWear is 40% off with free shipping, and you get 30 days to try them, more than enough time to put them through a real Vegas trip.

If they don't hold up better than anything you've ever packed, send them back

When you're exploring Epcot, Magic Kingdom, or Animal Kingdom, you're not just walking.

You're navigating uneven ground, cobblestone streets, standing in line for hours, and doing it all for eight, nine, sometimes ten hours straight.

Three things people overlook until they're already inside the park.

#1 that soft foam feeling doesn't tell you the whole story. (Put headline: #1 - Soft Foam Built for Running)

The soft foam is made to spring you during a run, but Disney means walking to an attraction, standing in line for an hour, then walking to the next attraction and standing again.

Because the line doesn't get shorter when your feet start aching from the foam turning flat

#2 Hokas, Brooks, and OnClouds were not built for cobblestone and slippery roads. (Put headline #2 - Non-Slip Grip Sole)

At Disney, you're walking across textured paths, uneven paving, and cobblestone-style surfaces for hours.

You want to feel steady with every step—not like you're wobbling over every bump.

Then an afternoon shower hits, and those same surfaces get wet.

That's where a slip-resistant outsole matters. You want a shoe with grip, not just a thick layer of cushioning.

#3, the toe box is narrow and it doesn't stretch (put headline: #3 - Wide Toe-Box)

If it squeezes your toes together before you've even left the hotel, that isn't a promising start.

After hours of walking in the heat, a tight fit can become the only thing you can think about.

Your toes feel trapped. You're loosening your laces at lunch.

And you've still got another park to go.

That's why Comfortwear built the opposite of a running shoe

Made for people whose feet have to get through real life. Nurses, healthcare workers, and travelers who want to actually enjoy the trip they've been saving for.

A roomy toe box lets your toes spread out instead of getting squeezed together.

A flexible stretch-knit upper gives your feet room as they swell.

Comfortable cushioning and built-in arch support work together underfoot, with a slip-resistant outsole for added traction.

Because you don't want to be the person cutting the day short.

Or sitting on a bench watching everyone else experience what you flew there to see.

You want to be in the photo. On the next ride. There when the fireworks start.

I weigh 250 pounds, and we’d been planning this family vacation for over a year and the thought of not being able to go broke my heart

But when you’re walking around Magic Kingdom or Epcot, you’re wearing the same shoes for nine, sometimes ten hours a day.

Cobblestones. Long lines. Walking from one attraction to the next.

When I finally went to the doctor, he just told me to lose weight and get surgery. That wasn't what I wanted to hear, so I knew I needed to find something else to help.

Now, let me tell you how I found the solution. I found a forum where other curvy girls were chatting. They shared tips that helped make their lives easier. One of those tips was the Ortho Stretch Cushion Shoes by ComfortWear. I was skeptical, but gave them a try.

To say they saved my Disney tip would be an understatement. From Magic Kingdom open to close, the arch support, deep heel cushioning, and wide, stretchy toe box carried me through over 25,000 steps a day without a single blister. My plantar fasciitis? Zero flare-ups. My lower back and knees? Completely fine at the end of every night.

I ended up ordering two more colors before the trip even ended. They look super cute with casual theme-park outfits, and they’ve easily outperformed my expensive Hokas and Onclouds at a fraction of the price. Now, they’re my go-to for theme parks, daily walks, and running errands.

The genuine cherry on top? ComfortWear supports initiatives promoting body positivity and women's health. Their values align with mine, making me proud to wear their brand.

And with a 30-day money-back guarantee, there's zero risk. If they don't transform your feet like they did mine, you can return them, no questions asked.

So, to my fellow curvy women who need more than just a shoe, but a lifeline for their feet: ComfortWear's Ortho Stretch Cushion Shoes are waiting for you. Trust me, they're a game changer!

## COM381 — Nurse Evil plantar fasciitis
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Net New · **Format:** AI · **Angle:** Nurse · **Persona:** Plantar Fasciitis · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** No Offer · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-27 · **Learnings:** —

I’m plantar fasciitis

Grace and I have the same morning routine. She gets out of bed, I stab her in the heel,

she grabs the dresser, and then she blames being 49.

Cute.

I live inside the thick band of tissue

Underneath Grace’s foot

That's the plantar fascia. It runs from her heel toward her toes and helps support her arch.

While she sleeps, it tightens. Then she steps down with her full weight and stretches at once

When that tissue gets irritated, those first steps after resting can really hurt.

That's when I strike. The funny part, after a few minutes, I usually calm down.

So Grace thinks she walked me off. That's my trick. She's a nurse.

Once the pain settles, she gives me twelve more hours of hard floors, stairs, and standing

between hospital beds. By lunch, her arch is exhausted, and she's limping back to her station. Perfect.

Then, during a break, another nurse noticed Grace rubbing her heel.

They got talking about what they wore on shift.

Her coworker pointed down at her own shoes.

Grace wrote down the name, ComfortWear

I wasn't worried, I was laughing

Another shoe that feels soft for five seconds? I've seen those.

Then I looked inside.

Oh.

**There's support underneath the arch.**

Here's why that matters.

When Grace stands and walks, her arch takes load, and the band underneath her foot takes tension.

A protective arch support helps support the arch under that load. That can help reduce strain on irritated tissue.

Which is considerably less convenient. For me.

**And underneath her heel?**

Cushioning.

So there's padding between that tender heel and the hard floor with every step.

Arch support and heel cushioning. Working together.

Then there's the front.

A wide toe box gives her toes room instead of squeezing them together.

And the stretchy upper gives a little as her feet move, instead of feeling tight across the top.

Those features are about fit and comfort.

The shoe started protecting the exact area i’d been tormenting

Her arch felt less exhausted

Her feet felt lighter inside the shoes

She started wearing them as part of her everyday routine.

But now, the shoes she wore on shift had support built in.

No more choosing a pair just because they matched her scrubs.

No tight compression socks

No bulky brace

Just her everyday shoes

Then things started disappearing: the limp after lunch, the hand reaching for the dresser,

That pause before the first step, and eventually, me.

One morning, Rachel's heel touched the floor

She stood up and kept walking

no stab, no limp, no me.

I’m plantar fasciitis and i don’t live here anymore

If your first step is the worst one, you don't have to keep starting your day like this.

But if you're a nurse looking for shoes with arch support, heel cushioning, and room for your toes...

They're called **ComfortWear Ortho Stretch Cushion Shoes.**

Tap below to find your pair.

I can't believe I just told you that.

## COM385 — If you’re a nurse, authority figure talking
**Status:** Editing · **Type:** Net New · **Format:** AI + Existing Footage · **Angle:** Nurse · **Persona:** Nurse · **Product:** Ortho · **Offer:** No Offer · **Month:** October · **Created:** 2026-10-27 · **Learnings:** —

Hook 1: If you’re a nurse and you have stabbing pains in your heel or foot pain like everyone else calls it, I have a warning for you

Hook 2: If you’re a nurse wearing these 3 popular shoe brands, I have a warning for you (put grid scene of Hokas, Cloves, On Clouds)

Hook 3: If you’re a nurse working 12-hour shifts with Hokas, I have a warning for you

So if you want to pull back-to-back 12s while working on hospital concrete floors without your heel, knee or back aching before lunch.

Here’s what I would do first

I wouldn’t buy another pair of premium Hokas or Clove shoes

Hokas might be the first pair someone recommends.

But if your current pair still leaves you uncomfortable halfway through your shift, buying another pair for the same soft feeling isn’t much of a plan.

Look at the narrow toe-box. They’re not meant for feet that swell after hours of being on your feet

Then there’s Cloves & Skechers.

That soft memory-foam feeling can be convincing when you first put them on.

But when you’re working 12-hour shifts, the insole ends up going flat by lunch under your weight

And Brooks?

Your coworker might love hers.

But if yours feel crowded across the toes by lunch, pay attention to that.

You shouldn’t have to keep loosening your laces just to make room.

Whatever brand name is on the shoe, here’s what I want you to look at underneath and around your foot.

Think of cushioning like the suspension in a car.

It’s one part of the system.

But you wouldn’t judge the whole car by pressing down on the seat.

And you shouldn’t judge a twelve-hour work shoe just by pressing your thumb into the foam.

That tells you it’s soft.

It doesn’t tell you whether there’s support underneath your arch.

Whether your toes have enough room.

Or whether the upper has any stretch when your feet feel swollen halfway through your shift.

Here’s the difference.

Cushioning gives you padding underneath.

Arch support for your plantar fascia to prevent it from tearing

And the toe-box of the shoe determines how much room your foot has around it.

Three different jobs.

That’s where ComfortWear’s Ortho Stretch Cushion Shoes come in.

Cushioning at the heel, paired with built-in arch support beneath the middle of your foot.

A wide toe box that gives your toes room across the front.

And a soft, stretchy knit upper that stretches across the top instead of feeling rigid and restrictive.

Cushion. Support. Room.

That’s the combination to pay attention to if you’re a nurse working 12-hour shifts

And when you buy two pairs, you get two free gifts.

And with a 30-day money-back guarantee, there's zero risk. If they don't transform your feet like they did mine, you can return them, no questions asked.
